# Val Levitan

Val is the principal decision-maker across the JVL group of companies. In the transcripts he leads the meetings, gives orders, and makes the calls on banking, product strategy, retrofitting, and shipping.

## Role and relationships

- Val is the person driving decisions in every JVL meeting reviewed. [[raw/cibc_account_the_january_6_2025.md]] [[raw/jvl_strategic_plan_and_vision_2025_2025-01-08.md]]
- Ina (Inna Levitan) is the majority owner of the JVL companies at 91%, with two minority partners at 4.5% each; Val, Ina, and Monica are the three account signers. [[raw/cibc_account_the_january_6_2025.md]]

## What he was working on (January 2025)

- Setting up US bank accounts with CIBC for the JVL companies. [[raw/cibc_account_the_january_6_2025.md]]
- Planning JVL's 2025 product roadmap in Jira with Aaron. [[raw/jira_value_stream_distribution_infrastructure_january_7_2025.md]]
- Working out the retrofit plan for used machines with Misha. [[raw/retrofit_january_7_2025.md]]
- Directing the shipment of 100 machines from the Mexico warehouse to Georgia, via Miguel and Mary. [[raw/shipment_of_machines_from_mexico_january_8_2025.md]]

## What he was working on (mid-January 2025)

- Chairing the FP7 hardware performance review and deciding to move forward with FP7 for the Flex-S cabinet / Fortune Fu Paws game. See [[wiki/decisions/2025-01-13-adopt-fp7-for-flex-s-fortune-fu-paws.md]]. [[raw/fp-7_performance_analysis_january_13_2025.md]]
- Chairing the development-pipeline debate and translating between the Minsk engineers' Russian/English while pushing for a decision. See [[wiki/topics/build-pipeline-parameterization.md]]. [[raw/development_pipeline_january_13_2025.md]]
- Making the corporate case for AI-assisted development — wanting a lead on the subject and framing the ~one-employee-per-year efficiency gain as scale-up capacity. See [[wiki/topics/ai-assisted-development.md]]. [[raw/ai-assisted_development_with_vs_code_git_and_mcp_january_14_2025.md]]
- Handling the Elevate financing call (Elevate is a lender — owner-confirmed; see [[wiki/organizations/elevate.md]]), explaining that price/description differences across Flex models are just hardware/software configuration on the same video lottery terminal product. See [[wiki/organizations/elevate.md]]. [[raw/james_reble_elevate_january_14_2025.md]]

## What he was working on (Jan 15 2025)

- Taking Elevate CFO Scott Rambeau's call to confirm machine item numbers for Elevate's pledge/collateral, and committing to gather the invoices and make a small amendment before end of February. See [[wiki/people/scott-rambeau.md]]. [[raw/elevate_convo_w_scott_rambeau_january_15_2025.md]]

See [[wiki/topics/test-automation-strategy.md]]. [[raw/integration_and_testing_procedures_january_15_2025.md]]

See [[wiki/decisions/2025-01-15-move-to-git-native-build-system.md]]. [[raw/parameterization_in_the_development_pipeline_part_3_january_15_2025.md]] [[raw/parameterization_in_the_development_pipeline_part_4_january_15_2025.md]]

## What he was working on (Jan 16–20 2025)

- **Game design (Jan 16):** reviewed art/music/math on several games, drove the "fat cat" persistence-game design thinking (inflation states, false-hit ratios), and pushed the diverse-market creative direction. Confirmed FP7 is sufficient for Fortune Football. See [[wiki/topics/jvl-machine-lineup.md]]. [[raw/game_design_january_16_2025.md]]
- **FP7 bottleneck testing (Jan 17):** chaired the review, ruled to stay on FP7 and optimize, and approved the Pixi 8.6 upgrade. See [[wiki/decisions/2025-01-17-remain-on-fp7-hardware-ceiling.md]]. A recurring theme: Val insists his engineers push him hard and stop hinting politely ("I cannot read minds… I have no feelings"), and wants a scientific way to measure performance. [[raw/fr-7_identifying_bottlenecks_in_performance_during_testing_january_17_2025.md]]
- **GitHub / DevSecOps coaching (Jan 20):** coached Andrei (with Aaron) on GitHub org structure and the ITSM operating model, refusing to write a rulebook and instead demanding logical "thinkers." He restated his method of recording every meeting, transcribing it, feeding it to ChatGPT, updating memory and building taxonomy — and his willingness to replace people who can't move past a 1990s "knowledge-tribune" mindset (says he already cut ~30% of the company and is doing better). See [[wiki/decisions/2025-01-20-github-orgs-mirror-value-streams.md]] and [[wiki/projects/jvl-2025-plan-and-jira-roadmap.md]]. [[raw/github_organization-jan-20-2025.md]] [[raw/meeting_various-jan-20-2025.md]]
- **Test-automation philosophy (Jan 20):** questioned a technical colleague to understand unit vs. integration vs. end-to-end testing and low-code tools (Catalon Studio), and set up a follow-up. See [[wiki/topics/test-automation-strategy.md]]. [[raw/test_automation_philosophy-jan-20-2025.md]]

- **GitHub access model (Feb 12):** chaired the check-in that settled repos private by default and least-privilege access; embraced Codespaces, the audit log, and activity-based access revocation ("the most used tool for me"); and pushed to replace the generic "IT" administrator identity. - **Sonatype Nexus SaaS call (Feb 12):** evaluated a cloud SaaS Nexus for artifact storage; insisted the next step is a technical alignment matching features to requirements, and wants only a one-year trial term. See [[wiki/organizations/sonatype.md]]. [[raw/sonotype_meeting_feb-12-2025.md]]
- **Meeting-tracking + file architecture (Feb 17):** described the AI meeting-tracking idea (tag subjects, watch a feature's evolution, GitHub/commits as the only source of truth) with his "$2,500 of meetings vs zero commits" cost argument; said he's indifferent to Office 365 vs Google Workspace; got Aaron's "engineer Val vs user Val" / publish-don't-sync coaching on storing files. See [[wiki/topics/ai-assisted-development.md]]. [[raw/detailed_transcript_meeting_tracking_and_AI_Integration-feb-17-2025.md]] [[raw/cloud_file_architecture_transcript_aaron-2025-02-17.md]]

See [[wiki/meetings/2025-02-18-git-lfs-vscode-onboarding.md]]. [[raw/GitHub_Commit_LFS-Feb-18-2025.md]]
- **Embedded group (Feb 18):** on the FlexPort/FlexPay standup, said his Incom contract is in its final stage and he'll arrange the production-server test; pushed Evgeny to allow a two-week buffer before placing machines. See [[wiki/topics/flexpay-mobile-app-and-service-advocates.md]]. [[raw/Embedded_Group_alignment_and_update_2025-02-18.md]]

Gave a stark **revenue warning** — he expects a **~40% drop in JVL's conventional Georgia revenue** from two new competitors, so failing to roll the product out across Georgia, South Dakota, and Puerto Rico threatens the company's ability to pay wages; distinguished market **rollout** priority from development priority. Also spent time teaching himself the correct hardware taxonomy (coffin/CPU box, HAL library, harness) from Pavel, and stressed the "looping" management discipline. See [[wiki/projects/georgia-market-rollout.md]] and [[wiki/projects/south-dakota-vlt-market.md]]. [[raw/Alignment_outstanding_subjects_2025-02-19.md]]

- **The Teams-visibility one-on-one with Michael Gottlieb (Mar 26):** asked to be copied on all game-development Teams chats and spent two hours on why. His **three stated goals**: (1) **count iterations and the dialogue between them** to expedite the process and "find an AI solution to communication," plus speed up file movement ("extremely inefficient" today); (2) build a **knowledge base from recorded comments and conversations** — "there is a scientific way of developing knowledge base out of recorded conversations… not talking about tagging" — to bring up young designers faster (Gottlieb: "You want to document tribal knowledge?" Val: "Desperately"); (3) learn **who does what**. He framed himself as "a transient passenger in this train… not a driver." Also aired: the experiment moving asset evolution **out of Jira into GitHub**, human-language interfaces to Jira/GitHub, the new **Unity MCP**, and a **five-year vision** of purely verbal, tool-nameless team communication ("the interface should be verbal and listening"). See [[wiki/meetings/2025-03-26-gottlieb-val-one-on-one.md]]. [[raw/michael_gottlieb_val_meeting-26-2025.md]]
- **Management philosophy, as stated to Gottlieb (Mar 26):** "I need to insert myself into everything… maintaining culture of awkward truth in a growing company is very difficult"; "I need to **bulldozer** through obstacles"; he must state things that cannot be misinterpreted; he is "fundamentally rebellious against large corporation mentality" ("every large corporation to me is the beginning of the end of life"); "I don't want to make you happy. I want to make you wealthy." On tolerating character: he has had **the same arguments with [[wiki/people/efim-podvoiski.md|Efim]] for 26 years** but keeps asking only "is there anybody better?… I will tolerate your character as long as you're contributing" — and demands the same standard be applied to himself. [[raw/michael_gottlieb_val_meeting-26-2025.md]]

## Background (self-described)

*(Personal matters removed — owner ruling 2026-09-28.)*
- **His stance (to Julian Puerta, ≈May 2024 — pre-window source):** "I am not interested in risky or adventuristic business… I'm interested in meat and potatoes"; he shrinks the business's inherent risk by being "very, very knowledgeable" and surrounding himself with knowledgeable people. On mentorship: he sympathizes with Julian's spread-thin ambitions as "the same mistakes that I made" when younger. He also recalled a pre-pandemic analyst — "one of the top analysts in Latin America," a woman mathematician who came from "sirsa" (as transcribed) — who ran his operation until pandemic salary cuts sent her to "FBM" ("fabama" garble, then corrected). [[raw/julian_puerta_jvl_mexico.md]]
- Negotiating with the bill-acceptor vendor, Val described his own history: **"I'm in the business for 40 years… I used to own a bill acceptor company. I owned an amusement company that made machines. I now own a gambling company. I own casinos."** (His own words in a negotiation — recorded as his self-description.) [[raw/igs_matt_mazarolli_restocking_charges-march-29-2025.md]]
- **The bill-acceptor company was named Cash Code (told to Aaron, May 12 2025):** while working the FlexPay payment example, Val said "when I used to own a company called **Cash Code**, Cash Code developed this device specifically for these needs" — the **bill-to-bill (recycler)** device that accepts and dispenses bills in multiple denominations (JCM makes a similar one). This names the "bill acceptor company" of his self-description above. [[raw/theory_product_discovery_process-may-12-2025.md]]
- **His route-operating history, told to a Georgia master licensee (Sept 8 2026)** — the most specific version in the corpus, given to explain why he found it extraordinary that Georgia locations pay a connection fee: "**I'm in this business from 1984. I was an operator in Canada.** In Canada, I mean, forget 1984. Even in the 2000s… **we had massive operations of machines in Mexico, Peru, and Colombia. Location never pays for anything. It's always the operator. The operator always pays. The master always pays for everything. I don't understand this.**" And on the split those markets ran at: asked "What is the split in those industries?" — "**You could never get better than sixty forty**… **Master always gets less because of the volume. Location feels that my location, my clients, it's me. I'm here every day**… **If you don't give them sixty percent, somebody else will come and do it in sixty percent.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Dixie_Amusement_Georgia_Transactional_Equipment_and_Bundle_Economics-September-8-2026.md]]
- **JVL's near-death, as he told Gottlieb (Mar 26 2025):** he once delegated the company to **two well-educated executives** — a childhood school friend with a science degree in statistical mathematics, and a former **Deloitte senior auditor** who had also worked for **Brookfield** — "and they ran my company to the ground." He says he brought in around **$6 million a year of his own cash "for years and years"** to keep it afloat, got rid of them, took back over ("then COVID hit and everything stopped"), and turned it profitable again — "I saw birth. I saw death. I saw rebirth." His moral: a company dies when the owner gets wealthy and stops paying day-to-day attention; his role is to prevent the cash grab, incompetent family appointees, and mediocre hires. He earlier built **a company with 1,500 employees**, and recalls his long-time corporate lawyer (still holding his books) teaching him that a company, unlike a person, "doesn't have to die" at 85. (All as self-described in the one-on-one.) [[raw/michael_gottlieb_val_meeting-26-2025.md]]

## Aug 2025 — Flex-POS iteration + the backend-architecture kickoff

- **Aug 6 Flex-POS mid-iteration sync** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-08-06-flex-pos-mid-iteration-sync.md]], with Nathan/Juan/Pavel/Genadi): reviewed Nathan's GitHub capability dashboard (9 "flex-requirement" capabilities, GitHub as single source of truth), directed splitting the ticket data schemas into separate Mother-Goose/Tito/e-wallet folders, asked for a location-conversion "differences list" and for **service-technician + master-operator personas ahead of the customer**, and reset the cadence (daily working meetings for the team; Val + Genadi in a weekly **Wednesday** review only). Same day, after a **call to [[wiki/people/john-houseal.md|John]] at InComm**, Val surfaced the missed **Prize-Card use case** (a barcoded Georgia prize card the player rescans at a Flex-Port-equipped machine, "renders TiTo obsolete"; John: Prize Cards non-rechargeable "for now," preserving e-Wallet as the cash-out route). See [[wiki/topics/flexpay-business-architecture-payout-devices.md]]. [[raw/2025-08-06_Flex-POS-Mid-Iteration-Sync.md]] [[raw/Flex-Port-use_case-August-6-2025.md]]
- **Aug 7 Player Behavior Simulator** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-08-07-player-behavior-simulator.md]], with Michael/Juan/Efim/Anton Leshkevich): after ~two months looping on why **Fiery Ice Link ("Gayocho")** posts excessive negative-win days in Florida, Val cut the loop and commissioned a **Player Behavior Simulator** — emulate player personas (bankroll ~$100, denomination, bet buttons, time) to judge whether **volatility** (not RTP — which is fine at ~92.4% vs 91.7% expected) is driving players away. Efim to build it in ~4 days (Python, fed by a 100M-game math-outcome file); Michael + Efim to build a parallel less-volatile Fiery Ice math model. Val declared JVL can build "**its own school**" of game design. Substance on [[wiki/topics/slot-game-design-principles.md]]. He then held a smaller Efim+Anton engineering discussion ([[wiki/meetings/2025-08-08-player-simulator-engineering-discussion.md|≈Aug-8]]) and circulated a Monday-follow-up agenda email to Michael and Juan. [[raw/Player_behavior_simulator.md]] [[raw/player_emulator.md]] [[raw/player_behavior.md]]
- **Aug 14 backend-architecture kickoff** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-08-14-backend-architecture-cms-transactional-hhr.md]], with Juan/Aaron/Will/Pavel/Nathan/Dmitri Chudinov): chaired the cross-cutting session integrating the legacy **CMS**, the **transactional products**, **HHR**, and **Omni**; ruled the team should first draw a **block-diagram of *what* the system does (not *how*)**, then carve MVPs/use cases from it. Framed transactional products as the **calendar** priority (July-1-2026 Georgia deadline, Jan-2026 beta) but not the architectural priority; named the driving business requirements (non-technical hot-swap, one-day ACH, immediate fraud lockout — "I actually totally ignore InComm's fraud capability… it's our own money, our own skin"); recounted that the "**lease**" taxonomy is erroneous (coined by Efim + Boris, JVL's then-VP of engineering, both barely English-speaking); and set a Wed/Fri architecture cadence (Juan/Will/Nathan/Pavel) with periodic recaps. See [[wiki/decisions/2025-08-14-backend-architecture-block-diagram-first-mvp.md]]. [[raw/backend_architecture-august-14-2025.md]]

## Jan 6 & Jan 8 2026 — "a hub, not a brain"; the Pyramid introduction

- **Jan 6 — Payment Hub billing & collection** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-01-06-payment-hub-billing-collection-workflow.md]], with Nathan Carver, Pavel and others): ruled that Business Central, not Payment Hub, sends the invoice — "The PaymentHub should be exactly what the name is, it's a hub. It should not be really a brain of any sort, or a database of any sort." On the 17-cent balance-check fee he argued checks may be worth running even on weekends, and ruled to keep the balance check in the requirement and "option it out" later if useless. He first said NACHA forbids a partial draw on one invoice, then agreed when corrected: "We could, but it opens a dispute possibility." He endorsed Pavel's cash-maximising invoice choice as "a great point". See [[wiki/topics/persona-driven-design-and-orchestration-platform.md]] (Jan 6 2026). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Payment_Hub_Billing_Collection_Workflow-January-6-2026.md]]
- **Jan 8 — Pyramid Technologies intro** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-01-08-pyramid-technologies-printer-integration-intro.md]], with Pavel and Genadi): introduced JVL as "a 42 year old company" that started in amusement and moved into gaming devices; told the familiar CashCode story (he founded it; sold "in 2006 to Crane", and "Crane bought MEI"); said JVL in Minsk is a 100% subsidiary JVL has owned "for the past 28 years"; and said JVL is "currently departing Canada", with headquarters to be split between Miami and a suburb of Atlanta. He called JVL a direct competitor to "Banilla" (as transcribed), asked Pyramid for a mechanical CAD file, and took the undertaking below. See [[wiki/organizations/pyramid-technologies.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Pyramid_Technologies_Printer_Integration_Intro-January-8-2026.md]]
- **Jan 8 (afternoon) — lunch with Rich Winley in Miami** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-01-08-rich-winley-miami-lunch-texas-distribution-pitch.md]]). A three-hour recruiting conversation with Texas operator [[wiki/people/rich-winley.md|Rich Winley]]; the Texas substance is on [[wiki/projects/texas-used-machine-distribution.md]] (History, Jan 8 2026). What it shows of Val: [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Rich_Winley_Miami_Lunch_Texas_Distribution_Pitch-January-8-2026.md]]
  - **His one measure of success.** "What matters is cash after tax in your pocket at the end of the year. Everything else is just noise… The only sensor of success is cash at the end of the year. Free cash." Free cash is money "your business doesn't need", not money for reinvestment or bills.
  - **Focus over breadth.** He told Rich to drop side ventures — "People in place is a liability… You're diluting your own asset" — with a rucksack story: he once walked "75 kilometers in the desert. with 45 pounds on me"; the weight feels fine at first and crushing at the end. And a joke from a Russian steel-mill owner about a billionaire who opens "a hairdressing salon on the side".
  - **Market theses.** Demand for variety is bigger than supply, so "even if another five competitors come into the market, who gives a fuck?"; "Locations never shrink in our business. They only grow in size." Over time "operators morph into locations and manufacturers sell direct" — except in Georgia, a "protected market" where "Vertical integration is not allowed". Manufacturers "obsolete our machines" like a new iPhone; locations squeeze the operator's share ("20%… Now it's 17… Now it's 10").
  - **Georgia, in his own words.** "I think that we just don't have the ear of a decision maker… it's a gossip society. We probably didn't do favors for the right people" — "That's my personal opinion. I mean, don't quote me." On data: "the interlock report is incorrect, because they go by tables, which is incorrectly marked" ("interlock" as transcribed; likely Intralot — an inference). He said JVL has "more capacity than demand" and he cannot say why: "Is it because we fell out of glamour?"
  - **The payment scheme in Texas.** On the Pyramid QR tickets: "I don't think it's a good system… But right now, I don't have to be a judge of the system. I just need to prove my machines" — and "I have a very serious, intelligent backup" from the bill-acceptor years.
  - **Biography, an earlier telling.** Operating "eight liners" about 40 years ago; video horse-racing machines "in thousands"; a US countertop business where he "started at two percent of the market" and pushed out a rival called "Merit" (as transcribed); CashCode "1990 to 2006", sold to "Crane", which later bought MEI; the grey business "died in 1996" when "it became a criminal offense to have a machine"; the Gaming Standards Association board in Las Vegas; "42 years" in the industry; 25 trade shows a year and 60% of his time travelling. Also the origin of the name JVL — see [[wiki/organizations/jvl.md]]. *(Personal matters removed — owner ruling 2026-09-28.)*
  - **Where the ticket-types errand stands.** At the lunch Val asked Rich what the Texas tickets are used for and concluded "for the three machines, all I need to implement is the QR code". Whether Rich is "JVL's Texas connection" of the morning's undertaking is an inference; no source shows Val reverting to Pavel, so the commitment below stays open.

## Jan 22 & Jan 23 2026 — the Pyramid NDA and prior-art disclosure; Clayton's engagement begins

- **Jan 22 — Pyramid NDA call** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-01-22-pyramid-tomislav-jeras-nda-scope-qr-prior-art.md]], with Tomislav Jeras). Val said he is "happy to sign" Pyramid's NDA but wants it "narrow enough to protect your particular application", and gave "full disclosure" of JVL's own QR technology (on-screen QR generator, TITO-printed QR, kiosk e-wallet) so Pyramid cannot later claim JVL copied it. He offered a brochure only "if you sign an NDA". Standing he cited: "we have been in business for 42 years"; "I personally owned cash code… for 16 years until I sold it to crane"; "I also sat on board of directors for GSA for five years" *(five years here, against "six terms" / 2000–2006 elsewhere on this page — recorded as spoken, not reconciled)*. His stated reason: "I don't want to get at the sunset of my career… I don't want enemies in the industry." Also: "I have no plans of manufacturing a printer or going into bill acceptor business." See [[wiki/organizations/pyramid-technologies.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Pyramid_Tomislav_Jeras_NDA_Scope_and_QR_Prior_Art-January-22-2026.md]]
- **Jan 23 — first call with Clayton Chancey** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-01-23-clayton-chancey-initial-ai-consulting-engagement.md]], with Aaron). Described himself as "not an engineer… I self-educate", and JVL as "not really successful yet. I'm still subsidizing it in ways." His history with Aaron: when he "cleaned" the company he took on the turnaround himself, "inherited Jira; I had no idea what it was", and through Aaron moved IT from local servers to SSO and cloud. His two-year goal: "make more games with less people." He set Clayton's first objective (apply Clayton's knowledge-work framework to games production) and first priority (the work of keeping Jira current). His working rules: "I do not read minds… tell me what it is in very binary form"; "I'm lost, let's park it" / "Great, let's continue". See [[wiki/people/clayton-chancey.md]] and [[wiki/topics/ai-assisted-development.md]] (Jan 23 2026). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Clayton_Chancey_Initial_AI_Consulting_Engagement-January-23-2026.md]]

## Feb 9 2026 — Diamond Amusement partnership + the field-intelligence doctrine

On the Feb-9-2026 Diamond Amusement arc ([[wiki/meetings/2026-02-09-diamond-amusement-partnership-meeting.md|partnership meeting]] with [[wiki/people/sean-fellows.md|Sean Fellows]] + the [[wiki/meetings/2026-02-09-diamond-amusement-exec-debrief.md|exec debrief]]) Val, in the meeting-summary bio, is placed as second-generation JVL leadership (~42 years; his father founded JVL in 1984; JVL launched **Cash Code** in 1990, sold that division in 2006; six terms on the Gaming Standards Association board). His debrief stance: JVL's $167 win-per-unit is "a sick number" well above the market, yet JVL can't displace 24,000 sub-average machines — so the constraint is intelligence, not product. His doctrine to Felipe: hearsay is useless; an operator must go **into locations and take the numbers himself** ("a pen and a paper"). He argued a machine offered at **$1** should win half of Sean's business, distrusts every Intralot/GLC report ("I don't trust any data we have"), and wants the partnership to seed multi-tier test placements. See [[wiki/organizations/diamond-amusement.md]] and [[wiki/projects/georgia-market-rollout.md]]. [[raw/Exec_recap_Diamond_Amusement-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/Diamond_Shawn_Fellows_interview-February-9-2026.md]]

## Feb 20 2026 — the JIRA custom-fields cleanup

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-02-20-jira-custom-fields-alignment.md|Feb-20 IT custom-fields alignment call]] (2:33pm) Val reviewed [[wiki/people/dmitri-kovalchuk.md|Dmitri Kovalchuk]]'s inventory of JVL's **165 JIRA custom fields** and agreed a **~55-field round-1 deletion** (cancel the $40/month Linksee plugin, delete two never-used Jira Product Discovery projects, 12 zero-usage fields, 3 Service-Management fields), with a **26-field "active" set** held back for audit. His governing reasoning: since the team reaches JIRA through the Claude/MCP layer, data should live in standard fields — hence folding the **Acceptance Criteria** custom field into the **Description** field (the Feb-16/Feb-17 migration) — and JVL should keep the smallest possible custom-field set, staying conservative about new ones and avoiding free-text labels. He also coached Dmitri on AI-prompting discipline: refine a vague Claude query by asking *by what criteria* it judges (after Claude wrongly called active projects empty), and open prompts collaboratively ("let's think together what happens if…") rather than issuing a bare command. See [[wiki/topics/ai-assisted-development.md]] (Feb-20). [[raw/IT_custom_fields_alignment-February-20-2026.md]]

## Feb 25 2026 — the Texas / Nebraska / Georgia FlexPort go-to-market strategy

On a late Genadi call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-02-25-genadi-packaging-texas-flexport-market-strategy.md]], 11:01pm) Val laid out his fullest strategy yet for taking the FlexPort payout devices to market across JVL's **three markets — Georgia, Nebraska, and Texas** — Texas being "the freest," essentially unregulated. He argued against adopting the Texas-standard **Pyramid-printer + Fujitsu-cash-dispenser** scheme: it would force a **broad Pyramid agreement** he fears bans JVL's own development and hands Pyramid a monopoly, and the Fujitsu dispenser must be **constantly (and expensively) refilled with bills** — an unmanageable service burden across a state "10 hours to drive across" (the same refilling problem already dogs Nebraska). Instead he pitched JVL's FlexPort + bill-recycler solution to a Texas operator as a way to drop per-machine paper/bill handling. His go-to-market: either **sell the kiosk** (operator buys only a dongle; ~$7,000) or sell JVL's own **~$800–1,000 dispenser**, and run a **two-variant show** — keep-your-Pyramid vs strip-the-bill-acceptor-and-printer-and-install-FlexPort (FlexPort's ~$1,000 given as credit against the machine; ~$1,000/machine saved × ~20 machines ≈ $20,000). He also described the **FlexPort online promo-meter scheme** (Facebook-driven promo credits that convert to coin-in only via winnings), sequenced **Texas → Nebraska**, kept Georgia as the Incom redemption-center plan, and leaned toward **card-dispensing over cash payout for legal reasons**. He flagged Genadi as the one lagging the plan and will convene a Pavel meeting to align the POS and FlexPay work. Full substance on [[wiki/topics/flexpay-business-architecture-payout-devices.md]] (Feb-25). [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]

## Feb 26 2026 — two Flex-dispenser planning calls (observes, then decides)

Two same-day Val/[[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]/[[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]] recordings. On the 2:35pm [[wiki/meetings/2026-02-26-tasks-planning-alignment.md|tasks-planning session]] Val deliberately **sat in to observe** ("I want to be present at your conversation, I'll mostly stay silent"), coaching the JIRA discipline — refer to tasks by number so Claude can rebuild them from the recording, keep tasks short (open a new one for a v2 rather than stretch the old), "close the past, plan the future." On the 3:18pm [[wiki/meetings/2026-02-26-jira-planning-flex-dispenser.md|JIRA-planning call]] he drove the design decisions: **generate the e-wallet in the JVL kiosk** and put **one bill acceptor + recycler + Fujitsu dispenser + printer** in FlexPay; **narrow the vertical FlexPay from ~2 ft to ~1 ft** in two stages; carve the features (**HFM 400** Fujitsu variant gets a bill acceptor, **HFM 401** the recycler — stop there); keep the **Pyramid printer** only for machines going to locations that already run a kiosk while JVL's own kiosk uses the cheaper **Tito printer**; and hand Pavel the **FP 505** "pyramid style redemption" rework. He also pressed the point that the **Claude↔Jira MCP must be brought to completion** or "you'd need a whole department just to watch Jira." Substance: [[wiki/topics/flexpay-business-architecture-payout-devices.md]] (Feb-26) and [[wiki/topics/ai-assisted-development.md]] (Feb-26). ## Mar 5 2026 — the North America ↔ Minsk communication breakdown (with Pavel)

On a Mar-5 exchange with [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], plus two same-day written analyses Val authored, Val laid out his diagnosis that information flow between North America and Minsk is **one-directional** — he pushes requirements/context/strategy down; Minsk executes and delivers code but the interpretation, questions and tradeoffs do not travel back. He named the missing discipline **"hygiene of alignment"** and his recurring principle **"I do not read minds."** After Pavel's pushback he refined it with Pavel to: **Minsk communicates internally but its output — especially undocumented architectural decisions — does not cross the boundary in documented form**, so the task is to bridge the fragmented feedback segments, not create communication from nothing. Full substance on [[wiki/topics/minsk-team-communication.md]]; cultural counterpart of his same-month [[wiki/topics/github-release-management-pipeline.md|Jira↔GitHub transparency thread]]. See [[wiki/meetings/2026-03-05-communication-breakdown-minsk.md]]. §15.8 clear. [[raw/communication_break_down_Minsk-March-5-2026.md]] [[raw/Describing_the_cultural_problem_in_Minsk.md]] [[raw/Mutual_standing-Communication_breakdown_Minsk.md]]

## Mar 9–10 2026 — designing the Jira↔GitHub branch-enforcement loop, then the transparency problem statement

Same-day calls (Mar 9) in which Val worked out how to enforce the [[wiki/meetings/2026-03-04-schedule-review.md|Mar-4]] rule that every In-Progress Jira feature carry a linked GitHub branch, then a Mar-10 call that reframed the whole thread around *why*. Substance on [[wiki/topics/github-release-management-pipeline.md]] (Mar-9, Mar-10).

- **Mar 9 — IT enforcement loops with Andrei Bosik** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-03-09-it-github-jira-infrastructure.md]], 3:58pm, Russian; with [[wiki/people/andrey-bosik.md|Andrei Bosik]]). Andrei laid out two nightly checking loops (in-progress task ⇒ must have a branch, emailed to assignees at 21:00 Toronto; a Jira start-date check plus a reverse check for **"ghost branches"** with no Jira issue), to run in a **Docker container on JVL's own Toronto data-centre machines** (not Amazon). [[raw/IT_GitHub_JIRA_infrastructure-March-9-2026.md]]
- **Mar 9 — Aaron's critique** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-03-09-jira-github-enforcement-aaron.md]], 7:14pm; with [[wiki/people/aaron.md|Aaron Bockelie]]). Aaron called Val's container design "unusually prescriptive" and a security risk, and steered him to a **per-repository GitHub Action** instead (branch-name must carry a Jira issue ID with a reciprocal link, else block the commit or the merge to `main`), plus a **central repo that flags noncompliant repos** on a trust-and-verify basis. Val accepted the per-repo approach. §15.8 clear (tooling only). [[raw/Aaron_Val-March-09-2026.md]]
- **Mar 10 — the transparency problem statement** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-03-10-jira-github-transparency.md]], 4:04pm, ~50m, Russian; with [[wiki/people/andrey-bosik.md|Andrei Bosik]]) plus Val's same-day written **"JIRA & GitHub Transparency — Problem Statement Draft."** Val separated the **problem** (engineering work is invisible — a management visibility problem, cultural not technical) from the **solution** (left to Bosik and [[wiki/people/aaron.md|Aaron]]). He pressed the "Story complete ⇒ code must appear in the feature branch" milestone, contrasted the developer black box with the measurable graphic-design review workflow, screen-shared the **464-open-task IP 89 dashboard** to show JIRA has data GitHub can't yet match, and framed honest questions ("why 464? why 8 days? why 20 iterations?") as accountability, not attacks. He would send Bosik the recording to extract the problem, fold in Aaron's proposal without over-complicating, and return a spec. §15.8 clear (tooling only). [[raw/Transparency_alignment_JIRA_GitHub-March-10-2026.md]] [[raw/JIRA_GitHub_transparency-Draft-March-10-2026.md]]

## Mar 11 2026 — two calls: Business-Central sync with Nathan, then the Flex Recycler Slim with Genadi

Two Mar-11 one-on-ones (§15.8 clear on both — no compensation content):

- **Business-Central / payment-hub sync with [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan Carver]]** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-03-11-val-nathan-business-central-sync.md]], 2:01 PM), a quick pre-finance-meeting check-in continuing the Feb-2/Feb-11/Feb-18 reconciliation thread. Val, "as a concerned business owner," pressed three things: whether JVL's **high micro-transaction volume** needs a **load test** (Nathan: simulate ~1,000–2,000 invoices/day, follow Microsoft's API-rate spec; MSBC should cope, the load lands more on JVL's own app); a preference to trigger **reconciliation per transaction, not per incoming InComm invoice**; and, most emphatically, whether there is an **architectural design standard for back-end audit-logging integrity** (sequential logging, gap = red flag). Nathan described JVL's safeguards but conceded the standard had **not been discussed** with the Minsk back-end team ([[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dima Chudinov]]/[[wiki/people/sergey-tsymanov.md|Sergey Tsymanov]]), still on Flex POS — so Val directed him to **open an email to Pavel (cc Val) raising the design-integrity question**. Substance on [[wiki/topics/persona-driven-design-and-orchestration-platform.md]] (Mar-11). [[raw/Nate_Val_Sync-March-11-2026.md]]
- **The "Flex Recycler Slim" design call with [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]]** ([[wiki/meetings/2026-03-11-flex-recycler-slim.md]], 08:31pm). Val worked a new **narrow, between-machines payout kiosk** he called "the most active device on the market," using a competitor "Silver Kiosk" as the design foil (its wide screen + wide base defeat the between-machines purpose). His rules: **≤1-foot / ~11-inch width**, head no wider than the pedestal, **≤15-inch symmetric screen**, keep the **perimeter lighting**, and a **smooth, gapless, vandal-resistant front** with mandatory **Gorilla Glass** and front-access service (no shelves/speakers/locks to collect garbage or be set alight). His economics: make the base **cheap** (~$1,500–$2,000 build via ARM + cheap chip, reusing existing memory) but earn **recurring transaction revenue** — a "bringer" placed one per 2–3 machines, sold at ~$5,000. He wants the board **simple/primitive** (pushing back on Genadi's reuse-JVL's-oversized-board idea), a **two-size metal pedestal** bolted down against theft, and the machine's existing **low-power door-monitoring mode** reused. Full substance on [[wiki/topics/flexpay-business-architecture-payout-devices.md]] (Mar-11). [[raw/Flex-Recyler-Slim-Meeting-March-11-2026.md]]

Val set the direction for JVL's custom Russian/slang transcription pipeline: keep JVL's **own slang-to-English taxonomy** rather than lean on a third-party tool; on Mar-19 he named the effort **"semantic understanding,"** asked Clayton to build it **as a GitHub-resident service** run past [[wiki/people/andrey-bosik.md|Andrei Bosik]], and ruled the **output must be precisely literate English with no transliteration** (input may be Russian; JVL is attracting non-Russian devs). ## Mar 17 2026 — writing JVL's own "Ways"; the automated company architect

A four-segment morning working session with [[wiki/people/aaron.md|Aaron Bockelie]] ([[wiki/meetings/2026-03-17-jvl-ways-on-github-setup.md]], headers 12:12/12:22/12:33/1:12pm) — Val's clearest statement of the automation he is building toward. He wants to shrink JVL (floated **~30% smaller**) by standing up an **"automated company architect"**: a virtual senior architect, pointed at a single **initiative**, that reads its **two sources of truth — Jira and GitHub** — and reports every deficiency (missing docs/ADRs, uncommitted code, features that don't link to capabilities/requirements). "It doesn't need to think out-of-the-box, it just needs to be systemic" — an alignment tool, not a decision-maker; humans stay in charge "for now." Aaron decomposed Val's draft prompt into a JVL **product-architecture Way** (persona + two-pass workflow + GitHub checks, with progressive disclosure), teaching Val the **way-vs-skill** split (his "project context / output format" sections are the *skill*, the guidance is the *way*) and warning off his capability-statement template. Val: "I am actually writing my own ways now." He ruled that **[[wiki/people/andrey-bosik.md|Andrei Bosik]]** should own JVL's ways ("trying to employ Andrei on this, but he's not ready yet"), not [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]]; [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan]] is capable but tied up until ~Oct/Nov. Plan: a next-day session decomposes the document into JVL's tracked Ways for Andrei to try. The requirements-vs-capabilities / INCOSE-grounding half is on [[wiki/topics/transcript-knowledge-base-tooling.md]] (Mar-17); the Ways/GitHub detail on [[wiki/topics/ai-assisted-development.md]] (Mar-17). Val's wry close: "I love how you politely said, Val, this is way beyond your capabilities." §15.8 clear. ## Mar 23 2026 — the data-access-strategy / CMS-cloud-migration decision

On a ~46-min Russian call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-03-23-data-access-strategy-cms-cloud-migration.md]], 1:34pm; with [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Chudinov]], [[wiki/people/dmitri-kovalchuk.md|Kovalchuk]], [[wiki/people/sergey-tsymanov.md|Sergey Tsymanov]], [[wiki/people/andrey-bosik.md|Andrei Bosik]]) Val drove a set of decisions on how JVL accesses its machine data. He ruled to **drop Tableau / Microsoft BI dashboards and the hard-coded-credential Excel-bridge**, to connect **MCP directly to the on-prem OLAP cube as a ~6-month interim** (role-based access deprioritized — only he and possibly Juan still use Tableau, and external casino reporting has ended), and set the long-term direction as a **full, all-or-nothing migration of the CMS/CASS stack to cloud** (PostgreSQL preferred over MS SQL subject to cost, LDAP→Entra ID SSO), assuming **Claude Code as the migration force-multiplier**. His own framings: piecemeal migration of a 10-year-old architecture is "stitching," and the two drivers are eliminating manual data-prep and having no staff to maintain physical servers. He committed to **compile the undertakings into a document from the transcript** and set a one-week scoping pass with a Monday follow-up. Substance on [[wiki/topics/olap-power-bi-architecture.md]] and [[wiki/topics/ai-assisted-development.md]] (Mar-23). §15.8 clear (data/cloud/licensing economics only). ## Mar 26 2026 — the languages / design-standards debate with Efim

At the [[wiki/meetings/2026-06-08-bank-of-america-banking-review-and-georgia-financing.md|Bank of America meeting]] (with Inna) Val did three things worth noting. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_BofA_Lenin_Hernandez_Banking_Review_and_Georgia_Financing-June-8-2026.md]]

He then laid out CIBC's terms from memory (see [[wiki/organizations/cibc.md]]). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_BofA_Lenin_Hernandez_Banking_Review_and_Georgia_Financing-June-8-2026.md]]

## Jun 16 2026 (Omaha, Nebraska) — two customer calls in one morning, the same opening, and the biography told twice

On his Nebraska field trip Val called on two route operators before lunch: [[wiki/organizations/jj-operator.md|J&J Gaming]] ([[wiki/meetings/2026-06-16-jj-gaming-nebraska-site-visit.md]], ~1h24m with [[wiki/people/rick-rees.md|Rick Rees]]) and [[wiki/organizations/funambulist-gaming.md|Funambulist Gaming]] ([[wiki/meetings/2026-06-16-funambulist-gaming-omaha-visit.md]], ~15 min, his contact absent). The market substance is on [[wiki/projects/nebraska-skill-market.md]] (Jun-16 2026).

- **The opening he uses on a first meeting, stated verbatim.** "I'm looking forward to establish special relationships in every market. What does it mean? It means that **I listen to the decision makers, I pinpoint three pain points for them, and I address it. And I make all the decisions in the company, so I can address it. Dennis, for example, cannot.** So that's why I'm here and that's why I meet with the ownership." The same logic, said to Rick Rees's face: "It's very easy to sell it to a **decision maker** because the decision maker is a **bottom-line guy**… but **selling to the operations people is difficult**." See [[wiki/topics/jvl-operating-principles.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Funambulist_Gaming_Omaha_Visit-June-16-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_JJ_Gaming_Nebraska_Site_Visit-June-16-2026.md]]
- **Two more tellings of the biography, and they do not use the same numbers.** To Heather Ackerman: "I'm second generation in the business, **my dad started forty two years ago**… He was looking for a job. And **that was the only job available**"; "I graduated from being **educated in digital electronics** at that time and joined them. **My kids are in the business now**"; one is an **attorney** "and she's taking over." To Rick Rees, hours later: "We were always a **Canadian company** originally… My father started the business in **nineteen eighty two, nineteen eighty three**, and then **I joined him in nineteen eighty five**. Now my daughter is our **legal counsel**." *(The corpus already carries "~42 years", the 1984 founding date and "45 years in the business"; these two June tellings are recorded as spoken and none of the variants is reconciled — the Funambulist telling implies ~1984, the J&J telling says 1982–83.)* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Funambulist_Gaming_Omaha_Visit-June-16-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_JJ_Gaming_Nebraska_Site_Visit-June-16-2026.md]]
- **The Cash Code telling, with "Crane" as the buyer.** "I had a company in the **nineteen ninety** to **2006**, for **16 years**, I owned a company called **Cash Code**. Cash Code was a manufacturer of **bill acceptors**. We **sold it to Crane**, and **Crane eventually bought Mars**. So we served as a **springboard** for them to acquire other technologies. So I sold in 2006 and I was going to retire… **After six months at home, I couldn't handle it.**" *(Later tellings in the corpus name the buyer as **CPI** — see the Sept-2026 entries below — and one names "CPI, Tesco". "Crane" also appears in the 2025 NTS walkthrough record. Both buyer names stand as spoken; **not reconciled**.)* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_JJ_Gaming_Nebraska_Site_Visit-June-16-2026.md]]
- **The Gaming Standards Association credential, used as a warrant.** "I sat on board of directors of **Gaming Standards Association in Las Vegas**. Was **reelected to the board six years in a row**, so I am familiar with all the gaming standards **because I developed them**." He then listed the product range: "we have **Class three** products, we have **skill** products, we have **amusement**." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_JJ_Gaming_Nebraska_Site_Visit-June-16-2026.md]]
- **Where he says the company physically is, as of June 2026.** "We have been in the process of **fully moving out of Canada to the US**… **Our factory has moved to Georgia, Atlanta**… we're **licensed in Georgia as a manufacturer**. But I'm not sure that we're going to stay in Georgia as a manufacturer — we'll probably **keep an office there**, but most likely we'll **move to Florida**." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_JJ_Gaming_Nebraska_Site_Visit-June-16-2026.md]]
- **He refuses to quote his own Georgia numbers — because he does not trust the data.** Asked by Rick how JVL does in Georgia against the competition: "It's such a difficult question to answer because **I don't believe any of the data that's coming out of Georgia Lottery**. It's not really straightforward data. They don't share much, so I also **cannot compare my revenue to somebody else's revenue**. So I cannot answer that question." This is the same distrust-of-reported-data stance recorded from Feb 2026 onward. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_JJ_Gaming_Nebraska_Site_Visit-June-16-2026.md]]
- **He concedes a number in front of the customer when the report contradicts him.** Having claimed JVL had the highest average bet at a J&J location, he backed off the moment the report was in front of him — "Oh I'm so sorry" — and the correction stands in the transcript as "**Bonilla is the highest and we're second**" *("Bonilla" is the transcript's rendering of Banilla; the diarization splits this exchange oddly, so which man speaks which half is not certain).* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_JJ_Gaming_Nebraska_Site_Visit-June-16-2026.md]]
- **A JVL colleague named only as "One".** Describing who had done the J&J installation, Val said this person "was his **VP of product ownership**" and "was **VP at the Seminoles for I think eighteen years** in Florida." **"One" is a garble and no name is asserted here.** *(Inference, not forced: the details match [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], whom the corpus records as JVL's **VP of Product** with ~17 years of Seminole Hard Rock operations behind him. Recorded as an inference for a later source to confirm.)* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_JJ_Gaming_Nebraska_Site_Visit-June-16-2026.md]]
- **Grace under a wasted appointment.** Arriving at Funambulist to find nobody expecting him because his contact was unexpectedly away: "I am buying it because **if he hadn't been there, he was a dead man walking and we want him alive**." He then turned the visit into an org-mapping conversation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Funambulist_Gaming_Omaha_Visit-June-16-2026.md]]

§15.8 clear — biography, market and product economics only; no compensation figures recorded.

## Jun 16 2026 (Omaha, closing segment) — naming what a regulation does to the man who has to obey it

Two lines from the [[wiki/meetings/2026-06-16-jj-gaming-bank-shot-and-nebraska-withholding.md|short closing segment]] of the J&J visit; the market and regulatory substance is on [[wiki/projects/nebraska-skill-market.md]] (Jun-16 2026) and [[wiki/organizations/american-amusements.md]].

- **He compresses a regulation into its consequence for the operator.** Told that Nebraska wants operators to check winners against a state debt database and withhold from payouts, Val's reply was one sentence: "**You become a policeman of the state.**" Same instinct as his habit of restating a customer's position back in a single line. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_JJ_Gaming_Bank_Shot_and_Nebraska_Withholding-June-16-2026.md]]
- **The amusement past as a credential, again.** "At one point, we had a support of **fifty percent of the entire countertop market in the US**. So at some point we **touched every single operator in the US**… Now this was **a couple of decades back**, but the name was quite good" — and on why JVL is not in it today, "**Because there is no market. There is absolutely no market.**" He also gave his itinerary: **South Dakota the next day, then Canada**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_JJ_Gaming_Bank_Shot_and_Nebraska_Withholding-June-16-2026.md]]

After the morning at J&J he took [[wiki/people/rick-rees.md|Rick Rees]] to a downtown Omaha steakhouse for about 78 minutes ([[wiki/meetings/2026-06-16-jj-gaming-steakhouse-lunch-rick-rees.md]]). **The raw transcript's speaker labels are inverted** — Val's lines carry Rick's name — and everything below has the documented swap applied; see the stub. ## Jun 18 2026 — the biography told to a fifth counterpart, and the carousel detail

Two days after Omaha he was in Rapid City at [[wiki/meetings/2026-06-18-mg-oil-dana-giesey-rapid-city-visit.md|MG Oil]], and opened by putting the same credentials on the table before pitching anything. Most of it repeats the tellings above; two things are new. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_Oil_Dana_Giesey_South_Dakota_Visit-June-18-2026.md]]

- **JVL the amusement company made carousels — the only source in the corpus that says what the amusement product was.** "**JVL at that time was an amusement company. Manufactured carousels. And carousels took a dive in the market. And I converted JVL, the amusement company, into JVL, the gaming company.**" *("Carousels" is as transcribed. Elsewhere in the corpus the pre-gaming JVL is described as a countertop/amusement operator and manufacturer; this is the first naming of a specific product line, and it is not reconciled with those.)* Also on [[wiki/organizations/jvl.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_Oil_Dana_Giesey_South_Dakota_Visit-June-18-2026.md]]
- **The rest is the familiar spine, with the Crane version of the buyer.** "a very large company called **Cash Code**… **I sold it to Crane, and then Crane bought MEI**", leaving "only two large competitors: Crane/CashCode/MEI and **JCM**"; and "I sat on the **board of directors for Gaming Standards Association in Las Vegas from 2000 to 2006. I was reelected six times to the board**… **This is what is now used in Las Vegas.**" His stated reason for telling it: "**The reason I am telling you this is that we know the subject very, very, very well.**" He also dated the family business here as "**my dad started the business in nineteen eighty two. I joined them in nineteen eighty three**" — a third set of joining years against "1985" (J&J, Jun-16) and "~1984" (Funambulist, Jun-16); **none is reconciled**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_Oil_Dana_Giesey_South_Dakota_Visit-June-18-2026.md]]
- **And the operator past he uses as the warrant for the Passport:** "**we were very large operators in Latin America**… we designed this QR code **for ourselves**." The product argument that follows is on [[wiki/topics/ris-remote-install-and-machine-states.md]] (Jun-18). **§15 #8:** clear — biography and market credentials only; no compensation figures.
